TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
TROY BOARD OF EDUCATION 
TROY, MICHIGAN 
TO: Board of Education 
FROM:  Barbara A. Fowler, Ph.D. 
RE: Agenda Memo for the Organizational Meeting, January 15, 2013 
1. ELECTION OF ACTING CHAIRPERSON 
The first order of business is to ele ct an acting chairperson who will 
conduct the meeting until such time as a president has been elected.  Past 
practice has been that the previous year’s president conducts this part 
of the meeting. 
2. APPOINTMENT OF TEMPORARY SECRETARY 
 The second order of  business is for the acting chairperson  to 
 appoint Judy Holmes to act as temporary secretary until such time as the 
 secretary to the Board has been elected. 
3. ELECTION OF PRESIDENT 
The acting chairperson declares nominations in order for the office of 
president of the board of education (please note, nominations need not be 
seconded; and a motion and support are made to close the nomination.)  
Please understand that four votes are required for election. 
4. ELECTION OF VICE PRESIDENT 
The newly elected p resident declares nominations in order for the 
office of vice president, following the same procedures as outlined for the 
election of president. 
5. ELECTION OF A SECRETARY 
The president declares nominations in order for the office of secretary, 
following the same procedures as outlined for the election of president. 
6. APPOINTMENT OF TREASURER 
According to Board Policy, the treasurer is appointed annually by the 
Board as a nonvoting officer.  The assistant superintendent of business 
services has served a s treasurer of the Board.  It is my recommendation 
that this procedure continue.

    2.   ORG    01.15.13

officiate the meeting until the election of a president has been completed.

     6. C.    01.15.13 
WHEREAS, there may be in and may, hereafter from time to  time, come into 
the hands of Mark S. Rajter , Treasurer of Troy School District, certain public 
monies belonging to or held for the State, County, or other political units of the 
State or otherwise held according to law, and 
WHEREAS, under the laws of Michigan, this Board is required to provide by 
Resolution for the deposit of all public monies, including tax monies coming into 
the hands of said Treasurer, on one or more banks, hereafter called bank(s) to 
be designated in such Resolution. 
NOW THEREFORE  BE IT RESOLVED  that said Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter , is 
hereby directed to deposit all public monies, including tax monies now in or 
coming into his hands as Treasurer in his name as Treasurer, into the following 
depository banks: 
Bank of America 
Bank of New York 
JP Morgan Chase Bank 
Citizen’s Bank 
Comerica Bank 
Fifth Third Bank 
MILAF (Michigan Liquid Asset Fund Plus, managed 
By PFM Asset Management, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank) 
PFM Securities, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank 
PNC Bank 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District may be 
withdrawn by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the Treasurer, Mark S. 
Rajter, and in emergencies the handwritten signature of the Treasurer, Mark S. 
Rajter, or the Superintendent , Barbara A. Fowler, who may sign on the General 
Fund account and the Payroll account at Bank of America; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District for the 
accounts established at PNC Bank  for purposes of paying Troy Career Center 
expenses may be withdrawn by checks with the handwritten signature of The 
Troy Career Center Manager, Assistant Manager, or Program Coordinators; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District f or the 
account established at Bank of America for purposes of paying remaining health 
care claims may be withdrawn by checks signed with a facsimile signature of  the 
Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter , and for checks over $10,000, the additional 
handwritten signature of Jasen Witt , Assistant Superintendent for Human 
Resources or Monica Papasian, Finance Director; and 
12
     6. C.    01.15.13 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District for the 
account established at Comerica Bank for purposes of paying health care and 
dependent care flexible spending claims may be withdrawn by ACH debit to Next 
Generation Enrollment, Inc., or by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the 
Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter or Finance Director, Monica Papasian, and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter , may open or 
close accounts with the above -listed institutions, provided it is in the interest of 
the District; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that funds of the District may be invested 
according to Board Policy D-1103; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer is authorized to combine 
money from more than one fund for the purpose of making an investment  
authorized by subsection (1) (h) of Section 380.1223 of the Revised School 
Code; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the funds of the District may be invested with 
the following institutions: 
Bank of America 
JP Morgan Chase Bank 
Charter One Bank 
Citizens Bank 
Comerica Bank 
Fifth Third Bank 
Flagstar Bancorp 
MILAF (Michigan Liquid Asset Fund Plus, managed 
By PFM Asset Management, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank) 
Morgan Stanley Smith Barney 
PFM Securities, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank 
THE MAXIMUM AMOUNT TO BE INVESTED with the above institutions is 
regulated by the school code.

* All meetings, except closed sessions, are open to the public.  The Board welcomes citizens’ comments.  The public participa tion 
portion of the meeting is limited to  one-half hour for discussion of agenda items and one -half hour for non -agenda items.  Each 
person will be allowed to speak for up to three minutes, except where the number of speakers exceeds the time limit.  In thos e 
instances, the President may reduce t he time limit for each speaker in order to accommodate all of the individuals who wish to 
address the Board of Education.  In any event, no one’s right to address the Board will be denied.

  A.  Senior Scholar Athletes, Winter and Spring 2012 - 2013 
It is a pleasure this evening to welcome and acknowledge our senior 
scholar athletes from winter and spring sports.  To achieve this 
distinction, a student must maintain a 3.5 cumulative grade point 
average while participating in an athletic program.

      2. C.   01.15.13 
2.  RECOGNITION       
C.    Board of Education Recognition 
Each January the Troy School District recognizes and celebrates the 
work of a very important group of individuals: The Board of 
Education.  
Troy is truly fortunate to be governed by Board members who are 
tireless, professional, and passionate about improving the  
opportunities of our students. Tonight, we would like to recognize 
our Board of Education  members with a gift  that illustrates the 
impact our trustees have on the lives of students. We are pleased to 
present you with a work of art done by Madel eine Chong, a student 
at International Academy -East. Madeleine’s ink  drawing, entitled 
“Transformation” depicts seaweed morphing into a beautiful fish. We 
chose this piece because it symbolize s the way this Board works to 
transform students’ lives with each and every decision made. 
Madeleine’s art teacher, Sandy LewAllen, helped to matte and frame 
this beautiful work for each of you, as a token of our appreciation for 
the hard work and commitment you show to the Troy School District. 
We als o have created a laminated year -long calendar, with photos 
showing Board members  with the students you so graciously serve . 
As you look at  this throughout the year, we hope that it provides  a 
reminder that our students and staff are grateful every day to have 
such a wonderful Board behind us.  
Thank you for all you do. 
Present artwork and calendar.

7.     CURRICULUM 
A. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice - Section 105 Elementary 
In accordance with Michigan state law, school districts are required to 
pass a resolution each school year indicating whether or not they plan to 
implement a Schools of Choice program for nonresident students who 
attend another district within their county.  I t would be appropriate at 
this time for the Board of Education to consider adopting a resolution for 
Schools of Choice , Section 105 for nonresident students who attend a 
school district within Oakland County. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy School 
District, exercising the option permitted by Section 105 of the State School Aid 
Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a 
limited Schools of Choice program  for the 2013 -2014 school year ; for 100 
nonresident kindergarten and 30 nonresident first-grade students who attend a 
school district within Oakland County. 
Yes: __________ 
No: __________

B. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice - Section 105c Elementary 
In accordance with Michigan  state law, school districts  are required to 
pass a resolution each school year indicating whether or not they plan to 
implement a Schools of Choice program for nonresident students who 
attend a district in another county, if that Intermediate School District is 
contiguous with the O akland Intermediate School District boundaries.  It 
would be appropriate at this time for the Board of Education to consider 
adopting a resolution regarding Schools of Choice, Section 105c  for non-
Oakland County residents.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution.

of Choice program  for the 2013 -2014 school year  for 100 nonresident kindergarten

 B. CONSIDERATION:  END OF LIFE COPY MACHINES 
  Twenty-two of our district-owned copiers have reached the end of life 
and are in need of being replaced.  Quotes and existing bids were 
obtained and r eviewed by Mr. Ken Crawford , Interim Director of 
Technology, and Mr. Mark Rajter, Assistant Superintendent, Business 
Services, and it is recommended the bid be awarded as stated below. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved By: ______________________________ 
Supported By: ______________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the bid for multi-function copiers be awarded to 
Konica Minolta in the amount of $ 250,796.00 and that this amount be approved 
as an expenditure of the 2012 -2013 General Fund.   The award includes a five - 
year service agreement component based on actual copies per machine, 
estimated to be an additional expenditure of $44,250 per year. 
Yes: ____________ 
No: ____________ 
94
Memorandum 
 TO: Dr. Barbara Fowler 
  Superintendent 
 FROM: Ken Crawford 
Technology 
  Interim Acting Director of Technology 
Resource 
 RE: Purchase of 22 “End of Life” Copiers  
 Center 
 DATE: January 10, 2013 
4420 Livernois 
Troy, MI 48098 
248/823-5090 
Based on building feedback of breakdowns and non-operable copiers, Merri Lynn Colligan, in 
the fall of 2012, began a process of identifying which copier models throughout the district 
were in the worst shape and which were the most costly to maintain and operate based on 
repeated breakdowns and/or service calls by buildings. Additionally, Konica has indicated to us 
that parts are no longer available to service these machines. The district has 22 copiers that 
have been identified as “End of Life” that are in dire need of being replaced. The district owns 
these machines.  
I sought quotes and existing bids by other governmental entities to replace these 22 machines. 
I located an existing bid with the Livingston Educational Service Agency (LESA) which was with 
Konica Minolta, our current vendor for the majority of our district copier machines. The LESA 
agreement is a five year pricing agreement which began in 2010 and is valid until 2/12/15.  
An analysis was done on lease-to-own vs. purchase of the models that are being considered. 
The result of this analysis was in support of purchasing the machines, since the total purchase 
price for 22 copiers is $25,000 less than the cost of leasing the machines on a 36 month lease-
to-own contract. The additional cost of a service agreement of .0039 cents per copy is the same 
for a 5 year purchase contract or a 36 month lease-to-own contract. The service contract 
includes all maintenance, repairs, and toner for these 22 machines for the 5 year contract 
period. 
Recommendation: 
Based on our analysis the Technology Department recommends the purchase of 22 Konica 
multi-function copiers (see attached schedule for models and locations) from Konica Minolta in 
the amount of $250,796. The award includes a five (5) year service agreement component 
based on the actual copies per machine, estimated to be $44,250/annually. The funding for this 
purchase and annual service agreement charges will be taken out of the General Fund budget.

 Preliminary Action Items 
Continue and finalize data processing 
review to include total cost analysis. 
 Cost analysis conducted on maintaining District’s current Finance/Human 
Resources software system (cost per student: $12+). 
 Summer 2012 TSD participated in demonstration of Oakland Schools’ 
Tyler Munis software solution and determined product would meet 
District needs.   
 Communicated to Oakland Schools, the intent to transition to Tyler 
Munis software product (along with 22 other Oakland County school 
districts) over the next 4 fiscal years.  
 Cost per student subsidized for first five years by Oakland Schools; cost 
thereafter, approximately $10 per student. 
 Oakland Schools still developing final implementation schedule with 
participating districts. 
 Final commitment letters from local districts need to be completed by 
late Fall 2013.

         B.      First Reading:  Revisions to Board Policy K-1303, Commercial  
                                     Advertising 
 Members of the Board Policy Committee have met to review and update 
Board Policy K-1303, Commercial Advertising.  The proposed changes  
have been reviewed by the Board of Education’s attorney to make sure 
they comply with state law, the Open Meetings Act, and the Revised 
School Code. 
 The first reading of the proposed changes  is presented at this evening’s 
meeting.   
K-1303 Commercial Advertising 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Ask a Board member to read this policy with the 
proposed changes.   
109
ADVERTISING & DISTRIBUTION OF MATERIALS IN THE SCHOOLS 
K‐1300  
K‐1303  COMMERCIAL ADVERTISING 
  No  commercial  material  which  causes  substantial  and  material 
interference  and/or  disruption  with  normal  school  activities  may  be 
distributed,  posted,  or  displayed  on  or  within  any  district‐owned 
property  without  the  written  consent  of  superintendent  or  designee.  
The  display  of  material  on  personal  vehicles  while  parked  on  school 
property  during  school  hours  is  permitted,  provided  it  does  not  cause 
substantial  or  material  interference  and/or  disruption  with  normal 
school activities. 
Further,  absent  the  express  written  consent  of  the  superintendent  or 
his/her  designee,  all  school  personnel,  including  contracted  personnel 
assigned  to  the  school  district,  shall  be  restricted  from  utilizing  Troy 
School  District  resources,  technology,  including  District  e‐mail,  and 
student/parent contact information received while engaged in duties for 
the Troy School District for commercial purposes.   
Adopted:  May 3, 1976 
Revised:  January 16, 1979 
  April 21, 1998 
  April 20, 2010
  June 19, 2012
